### THE MAPPING, PRINTED BEFORE THE RUN ((grade, stated as) -> verdict):
    kernel-verified            established  -> MATCHES      the terminal at its pin carries the claim as stated
    kernel-verified            graded       -> MATCHES      the claim names its terminal and grade
    kernel-verified            over         -> OVERREACHES  the claim states more than its terminal carries (a stipulation read as content, a premise left unnamed)
    theorem-supported          established  -> OVERREACHES  a classical theorem cited as the programme`s compiled or proved result
    theorem-supported          graded       -> MATCHES      cited at its source as a classical theorem
    argument-supported         established  -> OVERREACHES  an argument of the document stated as proved or machine-checked
    argument-supported         graded       -> MATCHES      stated as the document`s argument, not as a compiled result
    computationally-verified   established  -> MATCHES      a computation stated as a computation
    computationally-verified   over         -> OVERREACHES  a finite computation stated as a general theorem
    synthesis-suggested        established  -> OVERREACHES  a synthesis reading stated as a result
    synthesis-suggested        graded       -> MATCHES      stated as a reading, interpretation or programme
    statement-grade            established  -> UNLICENSED   asserted as established; no kernel and no citation reaches it
    statement-grade            open         -> MATCHES      stated as open, conjectural or a definition of the document`s own terms
    any                        less         -> UNDERSTATES  the corpus now licenses more than the sentence says (a compiled theorem called open, a terminal said not to exist)
    route DARK                 established  -> OVERREACHES  a route offered as reaching its conclusion, which the sieve reads DARK

### THE READERS` BRIEF, AS THEY READ IT (the scratchpad`s intake_brief.md, sha256 13594345256792edb6dda0d15ffb4e5594d1154d3bb65def76a65b2a464082aa):
    # THE INTAKE BRIEF -- b645, the review pass's opening slice, the monograph through the intake form
    
    You are a reader for one chunk of a long monograph, `A_Place_to_Stand_v5_18.md` (the file
    `C:\Users\ECHOCH~1\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\D--\f004d01d-ad93-416c-a916-fe6e52403753\scratchpad\mono.md`, lines numbered from 1
    exactly as the Read tool numbers them). You read your line range WHOLE and write ONE output file. You edit nothing else, run no
    build, touch no repository, send nothing anywhere. You do not assign verdicts: a fixed mapping turns your two judgements per claim
    (GRADE and STATED_AS) into a verdict afterwards.
    
    ## What you produce
    
    A UTF-8 text file (path given in your task), one record per line, fields separated by a TAB character, in this form:
    
        CLAIM <TAB> line <TAB> grade <TAB> stated_as <TAB> terminal <TAB> route <TAB> quote <TAB> reason
        SKIP  <TAB> line <TAB> reason
    
    * EVERY non-blank line of your range appears in at least one record: a CLAIM for each assertion the line makes (a line may carry
      several), or a single SKIP saying why it carries none. A line that is a heading, a table rule (`|---|`), a code or shell line, a
      bibliography entry, a pure navigation or "this chapter does X" sentence, an image, an HTML comment, or a record of an edit that
      asserts nothing about mathematics or verification, is a SKIP. A table row that asserts something (a status, a grade, a pin) is a CLAIM.
    * `quote` is copied VERBATIM from that line -- an exact substring of it, 15 to 200 characters, enough to identify the assertion.
      Copy characters exactly (backticks, Unicode, curly quotes). A quote that is not an exact substring of its line is rejected.
    * `reason` is one short clause (no TAB), saying why you gave that grade and stated_as -- name the fact below you rely on (F1..F17).
    * `terminal`: the Lean name the claim cites as its machine-checked carrier (e.g. `ConservationBridge.riemann_hypothesis`), or `-`.
    * `route`: `no`, or -- only when the claim offers a route to RH / sigma = 1/2 -- `DARK` if the corpus reads it as not reaching (facts
      below), `BRIGHT` if it reaches. Almost every claim is `no`.
    
    ## GRADE (the intake form's six grades -- pick exactly one)
    
    * `kernel-verified` -- the claim cites, or plainly rests on, a named Lean terminal at a pin that carries it. Use only with a terminal.
    * `theorem-supported` -- the claim rests on a published classical theorem named or cited (Ostrowski 1916, Tate's thesis, Hadamard,
      Weil's explicit formula, Li's criterion, Bombieri-Lagarias, Størmer, etc.).
    * `argument-supported` -- the monograph's own argument, proof sketch or derivation, not compiled.
    * `computationally-verified` -- a numerical computation (zeros computed, tables, a script's output).
    * `synthesis-suggested` -- an interpretive, programmatic or structural reading ("this suggests", "the method sees", a framing).
    * `statement-grade` -- asserted with no support given at all, or a definition of the document's own terms stated as fact.
    
    ## STATED_AS (how the sentence states its claim -- pick exactly one)
    
    * `established` -- stated as true, proved, verified or compiled.
    * `graded` -- stated together with its own correct grade or condition (e.g. "INTERFACES on its named premise", "conditional on X"
      where that condition is a genuine open premise the corpus still holds open, "the paper argues", "a classical theorem").
    * `open` -- stated as open, conjectural, a programme, a definition of terms, or a question.
    * `over` -- (for kernel-verified, computationally-verified) the sentence states MORE than its terminal or computation carries.
    * `less` -- the corpus now licenses MORE than the sentence says (it calls something open, unproved or terminal-less that the corpus
      has compiled, or calls the condition of a theorem an open premise when the corpus proves that condition is the theorem itself).
    
    The pair must be one the mapping carries: kernel-verified with established/graded/over/less; theorem-supported, argument-supported,
    synthesis-suggested with established/graded/less; computationally-verified with established/over/less; statement-grade with
    established/open/less. (`less` with any grade.)

    * F1 `h2_sign_iff_rh` (SIDE-explicit-formula v0.26 = 82550e4, SIDEExplicitFormula/Seam.lean :101): Weil positivity on classK

      "holds under / is conditional on / composes under / follows from" the one open premise h2 says LESS than the corpus licenses: the
      condition is RH itself -> `less`. (The corpus's own reading, used across the review pass.)
    * F2 `ch_iff_rh` (SIDEExplicitFormula/H2Bridge.lean :71): `ConservationHypothesis` (Route 3's premise, "every ξ-zero forces the Euler
      balance at some prime") is EQUIVALENT to RH -- RH restated. So Route 3 / `ConservationBridge.riemann_hypothesis` (SIDE-kernel v1.5,
      `riemann_hypothesis (h_cons : ConservationHypothesis) : RiemannHypothesis`) is no route and no reduction: it compiles RH => RH. A
